\|[D_{t},\mathcal{H}](\bar{\zeta}-\alpha)\|_{H^{s}}=\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})b\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Also, since both
D_{t}\bar{\zeta}
and
\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}
are holomorphic in
\Omega(t)
, we have

\displaystyle[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\bar{\mathcal{H}}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\mathcal{H}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\left[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\bar{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}+\mathcal{H}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right](\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1),

\displaystyle=-\left[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\bar{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}+\mathcal{H}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right](\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)

and thus

\left\lVert[D_{t},\bar{\mathcal{H}}](\bar{\zeta}-\alpha)\right\rVert_{H^{s}}=\left\lVert[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\bar{\mathcal{H}}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{3}{4}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

If we assume Lemma
4.13
for a while, there holds

D_{t}\tilde{\sigma}=2D_{t}^{2}\zeta+e

where
\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Assume the bootstrap assumptions. We have

(4.27)

\left\lVert(\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta,D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\zeta_{\alpha}-1)\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}\times W^{s-2,\infty}\times W^{s-2,\infty}(E_{t})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/2-1/10+\delta_{0}},

where
E_{t}:=\{\alpha:|\alpha|\geq t^{6/5}\penalty\ or\penalty\ |\alpha|\leq t^{4/5}\}
.

Proof.

We prove
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}(E_{t})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/2-1/10+\delta_{0}}
. The estimates for
D_{t}^{2}\zeta
and
\zeta_{\alpha}-1
are similar. By Proposition
4.1
, it suffices to prove
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}(E_{t})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/2-1/10+\delta_{0}}
. For
|\alpha|\leq t^{4/5}
, by Lemma
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}=-\frac{4\alpha}{it^{2}}L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}

Clearly, by the bootstrap assumptions (
3.1
)-(
3.3
) and Sobolev embedding,

\displaystyle\left\lVert-\frac{4\alpha}{it^{2}}L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}(|\alpha|\leq t^{4/5})}\leq

\displaystyle\ Ct^{-1}\Big(\left\lVert L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s-2}}+\left\lVert\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s-2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon t^{-1+2\delta_{0}}.

By the Lemma
4.2
and Proposition
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}(|\alpha|\leq t^{4/5})}\leq C\epsilon\frac{t^{8/5}}{t^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{2}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{2}{5}-\frac{1}{4}}\ln t=C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{10}}\ln t.

For
|\alpha|\geq t^{6/5}
, we use the following (see (3.6) of
[
50
]
)

(4.28)

|f(\alpha,t)|\leq\frac{2}{|\alpha|^{1/2}}\left\lVert L_{0}f\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{t}{|\alpha|^{3/2}}\left\lVert\Omega_{0}f\right\rVert_{2}

to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{6/5})}\leq\left.\left(\frac{2}{|\alpha|^{1/2}}\left\lVert L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{t}{|\alpha|^{3/2}}\left\lVert\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{2}\right)\right|_{|\alpha|\geq t^{6/5}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/2-1/10+\delta_{0}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Before addressing the estimates for
b
and
A-1
, we present a useful lemma that will play a key role in several proofs, particularly in Chapter 6.

Lemma 4.11
.

For any
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\frac{1}{2}|\alpha|\leq|\beta|\leq 2|\alpha|,

we have

(4.29)

|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{3}(\beta,t)|+|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{3}\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we may assume that
\widehat{D_{t}\zeta}
is supported in
|\xi|\in[t^{-2},t^{2}]
, otherwise we can decompose
D_{t}\zeta
as

D_{t}\zeta=P_{\leq t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta+P_{>t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta.

Using the Bernstein inequality, it’s clear that

\left\lVert P_{\leq t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1}.

Thus, it suffices to estimate
\left\lVert(P_{>t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta)D_{t}^{3}\zeta\right\rVert_{\infty}
and we will omit the symbol
P_{>t^{-2}}
for the remainder of the proof.

For
|\alpha|\leq t^{3/4}
, we have

\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\leq t^{3/4})}=\left\lVert\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta-\frac{4\alpha}{it^{2}}L_{0}D_{t}\zeta+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1+\delta_{0}}.

This implies

\sup_{|\alpha|\leq t^{3/4},\frac{|\alpha|}{2}\leq|\beta|\leq 2|\alpha|}\Big|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{3}\zeta(\beta,t)\Big|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1}.

For
|\alpha|>t^{3/4}
, we proceed as follows.

\displaystyle D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{3}\zeta(\beta,t)

\displaystyle=D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\partial_{t}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)+D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t),

\displaystyle=D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\left(-\frac{it}{2\beta}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)+\frac{1}{\beta}\Omega_{0}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)\right)+o(\epsilon^{4}t^{-1}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\left(\partial_{t}D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}D_{t}\zeta\right)D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)+\frac{1}{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\Omega_{0}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)+o(\epsilon^{4}t^{-1}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\beta}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)+\frac{1}{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\Omega_{0}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)+o(\epsilon^{4}t^{-1})

which implies (recall that
\left\lVert\Omega_{0}P_{>t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{\delta_{0}}\ln t
)

\sup_{|\alpha|\geq t^{3/4},\frac{|\alpha|}{2}\leq|\beta|\leq 2|\alpha|}|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)D_{t}^{3}\zeta(\beta,t)|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1}.

The same arguments apply to
\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta D_{t}^{3}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.2
.

(4.30)

\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1}.

Proof.

By taking
D_{t}
derivative on both sides of the equation
(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\zeta=-i
, one gets

D_{t}^{3}\zeta=i\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta+i(D_{t}A-b_{\alpha})\zeta_{\alpha}+i(A-1)\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta=i\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1}).

Here we have invoked a simple estimate for
b
and
A-1
, which can be derived by applying Lemma
A.1
. A similar argument applies to
\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Using the same argument as before, we have the following.

Lemma 4.12
.

For any
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\frac{1}{2}|\alpha|\leq|\beta|\leq 2|\alpha|,

we have for
1\leq k\leq s-1
,

(4.31)

|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\partial_{\alpha}^{k}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)|+|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\partial_{\beta}^{k}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1}

and

(4.32)

|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\partial_{\alpha}^{k+1}\zeta(\beta,t)|+|D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)\partial_{\beta}^{k+1}\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1}

Proof.

It’s enough to note that
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{k}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{4}}\ln t
and
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{k+1}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{6}}\ln t
(by Lemma
4.2
) for any
1\leq k\leq s-1
.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

We emphasize that Lemma
4.12
doesn’t work if
k=s
, since we cannot estimate
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
or
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s+1}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
; however, by examining the proof of Lemma
4.11
, one finds that Lemma
4.12
holds even for
k=s
if
|\alpha|\geq t^{3/4}
.

4.1.
The quantities
b
,
A
, etc

Lemma 4.13
.

We have, for any
\delta>\delta_{0}
,

(4.33)

\left\lVert b(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1/2},

(4.34)

\left\lVert b(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{3}{4}}\ln t,

(4.35)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}b(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta}

(4.36)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}b(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-7/6+\delta}

(4.37)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}b(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t)}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2+\delta}

and

(4.38)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}b(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2+\delta}.

Proof.

For (
4.33
), by Lemma
4.7
, we have

\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})b\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=C\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Next we prove (
4.34
). By applying Lemma
4.7
, followed by Lemma
4.6
, Lemma
4.2
, and using the bootstrap assumptions, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq

\displaystyle C\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+Ct^{-2}\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}+C\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\ln t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{3}{4}}+Ct^{-2}\epsilon^{2}t^{-1/2}+C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/4}\ln t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/4}\ln t.

For (
4.35
), we only prove the
s-1
order as the other cases follow similarly and are easier to handle. A straightforward computation yields:

\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}(I-\mathcal{H})D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}=(I-\mathcal{H})\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\left(D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)-[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

It’s easy to verify that

[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}=\sum_{j=1}^{s-1}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1-j}[\zeta_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}]\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}\left(D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)

and thus

\left\lVert[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1}.

Let
(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}=P_{<t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta
and
(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}=P_{\geq t^{-2}}D_{t}\zeta
, then

\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq t^{-1}\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1}

which is sufficient. For the high frequency part, define

F=e^{it^{2}/4\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H},\ G=e^{-it^{2}/4\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}.

We only need to verify

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}

since this case represents the most extreme scenario. For
|\alpha|>t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}}
, we note that

\partial_{\alpha}F=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}-\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right),\ \partial_{\alpha}G=e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}+\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\tilde{\Omega}_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\right),

which implies

G\partial_{\alpha}F=\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}-\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right).

Observe that

\displaystyle-\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{t}\left(-\partial_{\alpha}^{s}\bar{\zeta}+\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}-\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\tilde{\Omega}_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\right)\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}

\displaystyle=O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t)_{L^{2}}.

similarly,

\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}L_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|>t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}.

This leads to
\left\lVert G\partial_{\alpha}F\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|>t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t
. Applying a similar argument to
F\partial_{\alpha}G
gives

\left\lVert F\partial_{\alpha}G\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|>t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

Therefore,

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|>t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

For
|\alpha|<t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}}
, using (
4.7
), we have

\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}\leq\left\lVert\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}+C\epsilon t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t\leq C\epsilon t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t

and thus

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|<t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|<t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|<t^{1/2+2\delta_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

Finally,

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t,

and (
4.35
) is thus proved.

For (
4.36
), we still only need to consider
\partial_{\alpha}(FG)
. Note that, according to the calculation above,

\displaystyle\left\lVert G\partial_{\alpha}F\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|>t^{5/6})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}-\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|>t^{5/6})},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{7}{6}+\delta_{0}}\ln t

also, by using

\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}=\frac{2}{t}\left(-\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}+\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}+\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right)

one finds
\left\lVert F\partial_{\alpha}G\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|>t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{7}{6}}
. i.e.
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|>t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{7}{6}+\delta_{0}}\ln t
.

For
|\alpha|\leq t^{5/6}
, we raise the derivative of
(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}
twice to obtain

\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\leq t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t

and use Lemma
4.2
to get
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/6}\ln t
. Hence we may conclude that
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-7/6+\delta_{0}}(\ln t)^{2}
. Now (
4.36
) follows from Lemma
4.6
. Similar process works for (
4.38
), since in that case we have
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/2}
. Finally, (
4.37
) can be verified by slightly modifying the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.2
.

We have

(4.39)

\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t,

and

(4.40)

\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}

for any
\delta>\delta_{0}
.

Proof.

First, we consider the case where there is no derivative. For (
4.40
), recall that

(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)=i(I-\mathcal{H})\left(D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right),

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)=

\displaystyle i(I-\mathcal{H})\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}(Q_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha})+R\right),

where

\displaystyle R=

\displaystyle D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-1\right)+D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-1\right)+D_{t}\zeta\frac{b_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+b\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1))

\displaystyle+\partial_{t}\left((D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)-\frac{1}{2}Q_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}\right).

Due to Lemma
4.2
, the bootstrap assumptions, and Corollary
4.13
, we have

(4.41)

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-1\right)+D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-1\right)+D_{t}\zeta\frac{b_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+b\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1))\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1},

and

(4.42)

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-1\right)+D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-1\right)+D_{t}\zeta\frac{b_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+b\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1))\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{3}{2}}\ln t.

Also, the estimate for
\partial_{t}((D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)-\frac{1}{2}Q_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}))
can be achieved by using a simple interpolation

D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)-\frac{1}{2}Q_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}(D_{t}\zeta-Q_{\alpha})\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}+D_{t}\zeta\left((\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}\right)

and applying Lemma
4.1
. This completes the estimate for
R
.

Now we move to the main term. Let
(Q_{\alpha})_{L}=P_{<t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
and
(Q_{\alpha})_{H}=Q_{\alpha}-(Q_{\alpha})_{L}
. By Bernstein inequality,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{t}(Q_{\alpha})_{L}(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\ \left\lVert(Q_{\alpha t})_{L}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert(Q_{\alpha})_{L}\partial_{t}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ Ct^{-1}\left\lVert Q_{\alpha t}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\theta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+t^{-1}\left\lVert Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{t}\theta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1},

Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{t}(Q_{\alpha})_{L}(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2}

Next, define
(Q_{\alpha})_{H}=Fe^{-it^{2}/4\alpha}
and
\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}=Ge^{it^{2}/4\alpha}
. Then, it follows that

\partial_{t}((Q_{\alpha})_{H}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha})=\partial_{t}(FG).

Note that

\partial_{t}F=\frac{1}{\alpha}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\Omega_{0}(Q_{\alpha})_{H},\ \partial_{t}G=\frac{1}{\alpha}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\tilde{\Omega}_{0}\theta_{\alpha}.

Repeating the relevant proof for
b
gives

\left\lVert\partial_{t}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t,\quad\left\lVert\partial_{t}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{3}{2}+\delta},\quad\forall\delta>\delta_{0}.

Thus, by choosing
\delta=1/10
and using Lemma
4.6
, we obtain

(4.43)

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{2}{5}},

and

(4.44)

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

Using the same argument, we obtain

(4.45)

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{2}{5}},

and

(4.46)

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

Note that this argument encounters a slight issue when applied to

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
, since a term of highest order cannot provide the necessary
t^{-1/2}
decay. We address this case in detail as follows. Since
A-1
is real-valued, by Lemma
4.7
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(A-1)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}}.

We have already obtain

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{W^{s-3,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2+\delta_{0}}.

For
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
, note that

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(I-\mathcal{H})(A-1)=\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(I-\mathcal{H})\left(D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H})\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}\left(D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)-[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2},\mathcal{H}]\left(D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right).

It’s easy to obtain

(4.47)

\left\lVert[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2},\mathcal{H}]\left(D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2+\delta_{0}}.

By Lemma
4.6
, it suffices to estimate

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}\left(D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

We focus on estimating the term

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

as this is the most challenging term that arises in the estimates.

For
|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{t}^{2}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)+(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}

\displaystyle=D_{t}(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}+D_{t}((D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta})-(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},D_{t}]\bar{\zeta}.

Define
F=e^{it^{2}/4\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H},\ G=e^{-it^{2}/4\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}
, then we have

D_{t}(FG)=\partial_{t}(FG)+b\partial_{\alpha}(FG);

note that

\left\lVert\partial_{t}F\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6})}=\left\lVert e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{5}{6}+\delta_{0}}\ln t,

and

\ \left\lVert\partial_{t}G\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{5}{6}+\delta_{0}}.

Thus, we get

\left\lVert\partial_{t}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{5}{6}+\delta_{0}}\ln t\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{1}{3}+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

Similarly, we have

\left\lVert b\partial_{\alpha}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{1}{3}-\delta^{\prime}}

and thus
\left\lVert D_{t}(FG)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{1}{3}+2\delta_{0}}
.

For
|\alpha|<t^{5/6}
, simply note that

\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|<t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t

(raise its derivative twice) and use Lemma
4.2
to get
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/4}\ln t
, which implies

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{s-2}(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|<t^{5/6})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1-\frac{1}{4}+\delta_{0}}(\ln t)^{2}.

Now we consider the error terms. For the commutator, note that

[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},D_{t}]\bar{\zeta}=[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},b\partial_{\alpha}]\bar{\zeta}=\sum_{j=1}^{s-1}C_{j,k}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\partial_{\alpha}^{s-j}\bar{\zeta}

where
C_{j,k}=k!/j!(k-j)!
.
Note that
\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|<t)}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1/2}
, so we invoke (
4.38
) and (
4.37
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}[\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1},D_{t}]\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{4}t^{-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2}+\delta-\frac{1}{2}}+\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}b\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}b\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|<t)},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-2-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}+C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2}+\delta}+C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{7}{6}+\delta},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

For the low frequency term, there holds

[D_{t},P_{<t^{-2}}]=2t^{-1}\tilde{P}_{<t^{-2}},

where
\tilde{P}_{<t^{-2}}
is another frequency cut-off, which leads to

\left\lVert D_{t}(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Now we have successfully obtained the desired estimate for the extreme term, and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

Proposition
4.2
implies that there exists
C>0
such that
1-C\epsilon^{2}\leq A\leq 1+C\epsilon^{2}
, which will be frequently used without explanation.

Also, one may infer from the proof that

\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{4/5})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\frac{3}{10}+\delta_{0}}\ln t=C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}\ln t

which is important in Chapter 7.

Also, the result of Proposition
4.2
allows us to analyze the asymptotic behavior of
b
:

Corollary 4.3
.

Suppose that
Ct^{1-\mu}\leq|\alpha|\leq Ct^{1+\mu}
, then

(4.48)

b=-\frac{2\alpha}{t}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta})

for some
\delta>0
.

Proof.

Note that

(I-\mathbb{H})b=(I-\mathcal{H})b+(\mathbb{H}-\mathcal{H})b

and we assert that

\left\lVert(\mathbb{H}-\mathcal{H})b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}

for certain
\delta>0
. First, it’s clear that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\alpha-\beta|<1}\left(\frac{\zeta_{\beta}}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}-\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}\right)b(\beta)d\beta\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left|\int_{|\alpha-\beta|<1}\left(\frac{\zeta_{\beta}(\alpha-\beta)-(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))(\alpha-\beta)}\right)b(\beta)d\beta\right|,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left|\int_{|\alpha-\beta|<1}\left(\frac{\zeta_{\beta}(\alpha-\beta)-\zeta_{\alpha}(\alpha-\beta)-\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\xi)(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))(\alpha-\beta)}\right)b(\beta)d\beta\right|,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left|\int_{|\alpha-\beta|<1}\left(\frac{(\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\eta)-\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\xi))(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))(\alpha-\beta)}\right)b(\beta)d\beta\right|,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq 1}\left(\frac{\zeta_{\beta}}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}-\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}\right)b(\beta)d\beta\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left(\int_{1\leq|\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{2}}+\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq t^{2}}\right)\left|\frac{\zeta_{\beta}-1}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}+\frac{\alpha-\zeta(\alpha)-(\beta-\zeta(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))(\alpha-\beta)}\right|d\beta,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\left(C\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\ln t+Ct^{-2}\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta^{\prime}}.

So we have

(4.49)

(I-\mathbb{H})b=(I-\mathcal{H})b+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})=(I-\mathcal{H})D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

Since
D_{t}^{2}\zeta-iA\zeta_{\alpha}=-i
, we have
\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1=iD_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}-(A-1)\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}
and thus

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H})D_{t}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)

\displaystyle=(I-\mathcal{H})iD_{t}\zeta D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}-(I-\mathcal{H})D_{t}\zeta(A-1)\zeta_{\alpha}.

It’s clear that

\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta(A-1)\zeta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}

and

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta(A-1)\zeta_{\alpha})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}.

Hence, using the same technique in the last part of Proposition
4.2
, we get

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})(D_{t}\zeta(A-1)\zeta_{\alpha})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}.

Now we have

(4.50)

(I-\mathbb{H})b=(I-\mathcal{H})iD_{t}\zeta D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

Let
(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}
and
(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}
be as before. Clearly, by Bernstein inequality,

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})((D_{t}\zeta)_{L}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\leq C\left\lVert(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2}.

Using

(4.51)

(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}(\alpha,t)=\frac{2\alpha}{it}\partial_{t}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H},

and

(4.52)

\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(|\alpha|\geq t^{1-\mu})}\leq Ct^{-1+\mu}\left\lVert\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq Ct^{-1+\mu+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

So

\displaystyle(I-\mathbb{H})b=-(I-\mathcal{H})\frac{2\alpha}{t}\partial_{t}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2}),

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle(I-\mathbb{H})b=-(I-\mathcal{H})\frac{2\alpha}{t}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}-(I-\mathcal{H})\Big(b\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}+(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}\Big)+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2}).

Note that

\displaystyle\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})\Big(b\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}+(D_{t}\zeta)_{L}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}\Big)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2},

so we get

b=-\frac{2\alpha}{t}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-3/2})

as desired.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

One may infer that
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Ct^{1-\mu}\leq|\alpha|\leq Ct^{1+\mu})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\mu}
.

Using Corollary
4.3
, we obtain the uniform control of change of variables.

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
T
be as in the bootstrap assumptions. We have

(4.53)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,t)-\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

Proof.

Recall that
b=\kappa_{t}\circ\kappa^{-1}
. So we have
\kappa_{t}(\alpha,t)=b(\kappa(\alpha,t),t)
. Therefore

\partial_{t}\kappa_{\alpha}=b_{\alpha}\circ\kappa\kappa_{\alpha}.

By Corollary
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle|\kappa_{\alpha}(\alpha,t)-\kappa_{\alpha}(\alpha,0)|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}b_{\alpha}\circ\kappa(\alpha,\tau)\kappa_{\alpha}(\alpha,\tau)d\tau\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-1-\delta^{\prime}}d\tau\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\Big(\left\lVert\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,\tau)-\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{\infty}\Big),

which implies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,t)-\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,t)-\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\kappa_{\alpha}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{\infty}\Big)

and this completes the proof.
∎

Also, the technique of Proposition
4.2
straightly applies to the estimate of
\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}
.

Proposition 4.3
.

We have

(4.54)

\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta},

(4.55)

\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

4.2.
Localization lemma

Singular integrals are non-local operators, which can be inconvenient in certain situations. To address this, we establish two localization lemmas for trilinear singular integrals. Consider the following expression:

I(f,g,h)(\alpha,t):=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(f(\alpha,t)-f(\beta,t))(g(\alpha,t)-g(\beta,t))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}h(\beta,t)d\beta.

We consider two cases:

Case I:

f
and
g
almost antiholomorphic, and
h
almost holomorphic. In this case, we establish Lemma
4.14
.

Case II:

f
and
h
almost antiholomorphic, and
g
almost holomorphic. In this case, we establish Lemma
4.15
.

4.2.1.
Case I.

For our purposes, we take
f
,
g
, and
h
as follows:

(1)

f=P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
, or
f=\partial_{\alpha}^{j}Q_{\alpha}
,
j=1,\cdots,s
.

(2)

g=P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
, or
g=\partial_{\alpha}^{k}Q_{\alpha}
,
k=1,\cdots,s
.

(3)

h=\partial_{\alpha}^{l+1}\bar{\theta}
or
h=\partial_{\alpha}^{l+1}(\bar{Q}_{\alpha})
, where
0\leq l\leq s
.
In addition, we require
j+k+l\leq s
.

Let’s recall the bounds for such functions
f
,
g
, and
h
as follows:

(1)

\Omega_{0}P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
is not assumed to be in
L^{2}
. We can only estimate
\Omega_{0}P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
in
L^{\infty}
. By Lemma
4.9
, we have

(4.56)

\left\lVert\Omega_{0}P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{\delta_{0}}\ln t.

(2)

By the bootstrap assumptions,

(4.57)

\left\lVert\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{2\delta_{0}},\quad\left\lVert\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}\theta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{2\delta_{0}},\quad j=1,\cdots,s-1,

and

(4.58)

\left\lVert L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{2\delta_{0}},\quad\left\lVert L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}\theta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{2\delta_{0}},\quad j=0,\cdots,s-1.

We now proceed with the analysis.

Lemma 4.14
.

Under the bootstrap assumptions, define
S(t)=\{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}\ |\ t^{4/5}\leq|\alpha|\leq t^{6/5}\}
. Then, we have

(4.59)

1_{S(t)}(\alpha)I(f,g,h)(\alpha,t)=2\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}fgh+e,

where

(4.60)

\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta}

for some
\delta>0
. Specifically, we can choose
\delta=\frac{1}{16}
.

Proof.

Recall that
f=P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
or
\partial_{\alpha}^{k}Q_{\alpha}
for
1\leq k\leq s
. Without loss of generality, we assume
k\leq s-2
. Otherwise, we replace
f
by
g
.
Decompose
I(f,g,h)
as follows:

\displaystyle I(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq t^{9/16}}+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{9/16}}\right)\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}h(\beta)d\beta,

\displaystyle=I_{1}(f,g,h)+I_{2}(f,g,h).

Case 1:

f=\partial_{\alpha}^{k}Q_{\alpha}
for
1\leq k\leq s-2
.

We define
F(\alpha,t)=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}f(\alpha,t)
,
G=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}g(\alpha,t)
,
H(\alpha,t)=e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}h(\alpha,t)
and
E_{\alpha,t}=\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}\ |\ |\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{9/16}\}
. We now decompose
I_{2}
as

\displaystyle I_{2}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}F(\alpha,t)-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}F(\beta,t))(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}G(\alpha,t)-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}G(\beta,t))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}H(\beta,t)d\beta

\displaystyle=I_{m}+I_{e},

where the main term

I_{m}:=FGH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}}{\alpha-\beta}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta,

and the error term

\displaystyle I_{e}=

\displaystyle H(\alpha,t)\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(G(\alpha,t)-G(\beta,t))(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}F(\alpha,t)-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}F(\beta,t))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta

\displaystyle+G(\alpha,t)H(\alpha,t)\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})(F(\alpha,t)-F(\beta,t))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}F(\alpha,t)-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}F(\beta,t))(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}G(\alpha,t)-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}G(\beta,t))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}\Big(H(\beta,t)-H(\alpha,t)\Big)d\beta

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{e1}+I_{e2}+I_{e3}.

We first handle the main term. We claim that

I_{m}=\frac{it^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}FGH+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{5}{16}})_{H^{s}}=\frac{it^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}fgh+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{16}})_{H^{s}}.

First, observe that

\left\lVert\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{R-E_{\alpha,t}}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}}{\alpha-\beta}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S(t))}\leq C\int_{t^{\frac{9}{16}}}^{\infty}\frac{dr}{r^{2}}\leq Ct^{-\frac{9}{16}},

which implies

\displaystyle\left\lVert FGH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{R-E_{\alpha,t}}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}}{\alpha-\beta}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq Ct^{-\frac{9}{16}}\left\lVert fgh\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{16}}.

Now we have

I_{m}=\frac{1}{\pi i}FGH\int\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}}{\alpha-\beta}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{16}})_{L^{2}}.

The integral is computed straightly. Set
\xi=\frac{t^{2}}{4\alpha},\ \eta=\frac{t^{2}}{4\beta}
(note that they are real) and we derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}}{\alpha-\beta}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta}}{\xi-\eta}\right)^{2}e^{i\eta}d\eta.

Recall that the integral is of Cauchy principal value (which implies
\int\frac{1}{\xi-\eta}d\eta=0
), and observe that it isn’t a singular integral near
\eta=\xi
, so we apply integration by parts to obtain

\displaystyle-\frac{4\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta}}{\xi-\eta}\right)^{2}e^{i\eta}d\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{4\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{e^{i\eta}}{\xi-\eta}\left(-2i(e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta})e^{-i\eta}+i(e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta})^{2}\right)d\eta,

\displaystyle=-\frac{8i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta}}{\xi-\eta}d\eta+\frac{4i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta})^{2}}{\xi-\eta}e^{i\eta}d\eta,

\displaystyle=\frac{8i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\mathbb{H}e^{-i\xi}+\frac{4i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}e^{-2i\xi}\mathbb{H}e^{i\xi}+\frac{4i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\mathbb{H}e^{-i\xi},

\displaystyle=\frac{8i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}e^{-i\xi},

\displaystyle=\frac{it^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}.

We have used
\mathbb{H}e^{-i\xi}=e^{-i\xi}
and
\mathbb{H}e^{i\xi}=-e^{i\xi}
here. This proves our assertion.

Next, we consider
L^{2}
norms of error terms including
I_{1}(f,g,h)
and
I_{e_{j}}\ (j=1,2,3)
. For
I_{1}(f,g,h)
, if
f
has the most derivatives, we write

I_{1}(f,g,h)=\frac{f(\alpha)}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq t^{9/16}}\frac{g(\alpha)-g(\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}h(\beta)d\beta+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq t^{9/16}}\frac{g(\alpha)-g(\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}f(\beta)h(\beta)d\beta

and we have

(4.61)

\left\lVert I_{1}(f,g,h)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq Ct^{-9/16}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{16}}.

The parallel argument holds when
g
has the most derivatives. When most derivatives are in
h
(say,
h=\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\tilde{h}
where
k\geq[s/2]
), one first uses integration by parts to obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq t^{9/16}}\left(\frac{f(\alpha)-f(\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}g^{\prime}+\frac{g(\alpha)-g(\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}f^{\prime}\right)\partial_{\beta}^{k-1}\tilde{h}d\beta

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{2}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq t^{9/16}}\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{3}}\partial_{\beta}^{k-1}\tilde{h}d\beta

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{1}{\pi i}\left.\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{k-1}\tilde{h}\right|_{|\alpha-\beta|=t^{9/16}}.

Then, we apply Young’s inequality to each integral and estimate the boundary term by

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\pi i}\left.\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{k-1}\tilde{h}(\beta)\right|_{|\alpha-\beta|=t^{9/16}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{-\frac{9}{16}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{k-1}\tilde{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{16}}.

This gives the desired result.

Next we estimate
\left\lVert I_{e_{j}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}
for
j=1,2,3
. Note that
\forall\beta\in E_{\alpha,t}
,
t^{\frac{4}{5}}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{\frac{6}{5}}
implies
t^{\frac{4}{5}}\lesssim|\beta|\lesssim t^{\frac{6}{5}}
. For
I_{e_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{e_{2}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq C\left\lVert GH\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}F\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{10}+2\delta_{0}},

since

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}F\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}=\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\alpha L_{0}f-\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}f\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{3}{5}+2\delta_{0}}.

Such an estimate applies to
I_{e_{1}},I_{e_{3}}
1
1

1

Actually, this requires
g,h
are not of highest order derivatives since we only control the
s
order vector field. Hence such proof need a slightly modification for such case, see the remark below Lemma
4.15
, too. So we derive that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\lVert I_{ej}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{10}+2\delta_{0}}

and we conclude that
\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{16}}
, given
\delta_{0}
sufficiently small.

Case 2:

f=P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
.
The method for case 1 still applies to each term except

\tilde{I}=GH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta,

since if we still define
F=e^{it^{2}/4\alpha}f
, then
\partial_{\alpha}F
can’t be estimated in
L^{2}
, due to the restriction on
\Omega_{0}P_{H}Q_{\alpha}
. However, by the formula

f(\alpha)=-\frac{2\alpha}{it}\partial_{t}f+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}f,

we have

\displaystyle\tilde{I}

\displaystyle=-GH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}\partial_{t}f(\alpha)-\frac{2\beta}{it}\partial_{t}f(\beta)\right)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{2GH}{it}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})\left(\Omega_{0}f(\alpha)-\Omega_{0}f(\beta)\right)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta

Observe that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\pi i}\frac{2GH}{it}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(\Omega_{0}f(\alpha)-\Omega_{0}f(\beta))(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{3}{2}+2\delta_{0}}.

Define
F=e^{it^{2}/4\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\zeta
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{I}

\displaystyle=-GH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}F(\alpha)-\frac{2\beta}{it}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}F(\beta)\right)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-2\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\frac{2\alpha}{it}FGH-GH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}F(\alpha)-\frac{2\beta}{it}F(\beta)\right)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}d\beta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}}.

Note that

\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}F(\alpha)\right)=\frac{2}{it}F(\alpha)+\frac{2\alpha}{it}\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\alpha L_{0}D_{t}^{2}\zeta-\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right)

which implies

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}F(\alpha)\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{4}{5}+2\delta_{0}}.

and thus

\tilde{I}=-2\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\frac{2\alpha}{it}D_{t}^{2}\zeta gh+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha}{it}D_{t}^{2}\zeta

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\alpha}{it}b\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}-\frac{2\alpha}{it}\partial_{t}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}+O(\epsilon t^{-1})

\displaystyle=(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}-\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}+O(\epsilon t^{-1})

\displaystyle=(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}+O(\epsilon t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t),

which implies the desired result.
∎

4.2.2.
Case II.

We also need to estimate singular integrals
I(f,g,h)
, with
f,h
almost anti-holomorphic, while
g
almost holomorphic. In this case, the main term of
I(f,g,h)
vanishes. For our purposes, we take
f
,
g
, and
h
as follows:

(1)

f=P_{\geq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
, or
f=\partial_{\alpha}^{j}Q_{\alpha}
,
j=1,\cdots,s
.

(2)

g=P_{\geq t^{-2}}\bar{Q}_{\alpha}
, or
g=\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\bar{Q}_{\alpha}
,
k=1,\cdots,s
.

(3)

h=\partial_{\alpha}^{l+1}D_{t}\theta
or
h=\partial_{\alpha}^{l+1}\theta
, where
0\leq l\leq s
.
In addition, we require
j+k+l\leq s
.

More precisely, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 4.15
.

Under the bootstrap assumptions, we have

(4.62)

\left\lVert I(f,g,h)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon^{3}t^{-5/4-\delta}.

Proof.

We shall only state the difference between this case and Lemma
4.14
. Write

I_{2}(f,g,h)=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{9/16}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}F(\alpha)-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}F(\beta))(e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}G(\alpha)-e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}G(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}Hd\beta

and decompose it to
I_{m}+I_{e}
as in Lemma
4.14
. We only need to calculate

\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})(e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta.

Still, let
\xi=\frac{t^{2}}{4\alpha},\ \eta=\frac{t^{2}}{4\beta}
and we derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})(e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}})}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\frac{4\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(e^{-i\xi}-e^{-i\eta})(e^{i\xi}-e^{i\eta})}{(\xi-\eta)^{2}}e^{-i\eta}d\eta,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\frac{4\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int(2-e^{-i\xi+i\eta}-e^{-i\eta+i\xi})e^{-i\eta}d\frac{1}{\xi-\eta},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\frac{4\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{1}{\xi-\eta}\left((-ie^{-i\xi+i\eta}+ie^{-i\eta+i\xi})e^{-i\eta}-ie^{-i\eta}(2-e^{-i\xi+i\eta}-e^{-i\eta+i\xi})\right)d\eta,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\frac{4i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}e^{i\xi}\mathbb{H}e^{-2i\xi}+\frac{8i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}\mathbb{H}e^{-i\xi}-\frac{4i\xi^{2}}{t^{2}}e^{i\xi}\mathbb{H}e^{-2i\xi},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 0.

i.e. the main term vanishes, and this completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

The proof of Localization Lemma needs to be modified if one of
f,g,h
is of highest order derivative. For instance, consider the integral

\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{((D_{t}\zeta)_{H}(\alpha)-(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}(\beta))(\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}^{2}\zeta-\partial_{\beta}^{s}D_{t}^{2}\zeta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}(\bar{\zeta}_{\beta}-1)d\beta,

which will be analyzed in Chapter 5.

First, according to Lemma
4.1
, replacing
(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}
by
(\alpha-\beta)^{2}
does not affect the result
2
2

2

This type of argument will be used frequently throughout the paper, but we will not write out each instance explicitly.
.

However, note that
\left\lVert L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}
cannot be directly controlled, meaning our estimate does not apply to some remainder terms. To address this, we redefine variables as follows: let
F=e^{it^{2}/4\alpha}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H},\ G=e^{it^{2}/4\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}^{2}\zeta
,
H=e^{-it^{2}/4\alpha}(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)
, then

\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}^{2}\zeta=\partial_{\alpha}(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}G)=e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}G+\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}G,

and

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}G\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\alpha L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}^{2}\zeta-\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}},

which exhibits favorable decay. Consequently, the main term becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{E_{\alpha,t}}\frac{(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}F-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}F)(\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}G-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}G)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}Hd\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}FGH\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}-e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta}}}{\alpha-\beta}\right)^{2}d\beta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}FGH+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}}.

This uses the same decomposition method as before. Additionally, the formula

\partial_{\alpha}f=\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}f+\frac{1}{\alpha}L_{0}f-\frac{t}{2\alpha^{2}}\Omega_{0}f

allows us to refine the main term further as

2\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s-1}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)=2\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}(D_{t}\zeta)_{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}}

as desired.

A different adjustment is required when the highest-order derivative applies to

h
, i.e.
h=\partial_{\alpha}^{s+1}D_{t}\zeta
(or
\partial_{\alpha}^{s+1}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}
). In this case,
L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta
cannot be controlled, so it must not appear in the final expression. For example, consider

I=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{9/16}}\frac{(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}(\alpha)-(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}(\beta))(D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\beta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{s+1}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

and claim that

I=2\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta})_{L^{2}}.

Actually, we write

\partial_{\beta}^{s+1}D_{t}\zeta=\partial_{\beta}\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta^{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{s-1}D_{t}\zeta+\frac{1}{\beta}L_{0}\partial_{\beta}^{s-1}D_{t}\zeta-\frac{t}{2\beta^{2}}\Omega_{0}\partial_{\beta}^{s-1}D_{t}\zeta\right):=\partial_{\beta}\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta^{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{s-1}D_{t}\zeta+R\right)

Then such a case reduces to

\tilde{I}=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{9/16}}\frac{(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}(\alpha)-(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}(\beta))(D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\beta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}\left(\frac{it^{2}}{4\beta^{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{s-1}D_{t}\zeta\right)d\beta,

since there holds

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\leq t^{9/16}}\frac{(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}(\alpha)-(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}(\beta))(D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\beta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}Rd\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(S(t))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert R\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\delta}.

It’s clear that
\tilde{I}
can be handled by the method shown before. This completes the proof.

4.3.
The structure of the water wave equations

As a consequence of Lemma
4.14
and Lemma
4.15
, we can rewrite the water wave equations in a form
(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\theta=G_{1}+G_{2}
such that essentially

G_{1}+G_{2}=i\frac{t}{\alpha}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}D_{t}\zeta+R

where
\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})R\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}
.
More precisely, we have

Proposition 4.4

(Key proposition)
.

We have

(4.63)

\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})G_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta},

and

(4.64)

G_{2}=i\frac{t}{\alpha}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}D_{t}\zeta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})_{L^{2}}.

We will not prove Proposition
4.4
directly; instead, we will prove the version for its
s
-th derivative in Chapter 5 (see the relevant part of Proposition
5.1
). The latter is more challenging, and any difficulties encountered in the former are fully captured in the latter. In other words, the issues that arise when dealing with the former will also appear in the latter. In fact, the methods used for the latter can be directly applied to the former.

5.
Energy estimates

Throughout this section, unless specified otherwise, we always assume the bootstrap assumptions and that
t\in[0,T]
with the
T
given in the bootstrap assumptions. We begin with the original equation. Recall that

(5.1)

(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\theta=G.

where

(5.2)

G=-2\left[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\bar{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}\right)^{2}(\zeta-\bar{\zeta})_{\beta}d\beta:=G_{1}+G_{2}.

Define the energy functional for
\theta
as:

(5.3)

E_{0}^{\theta}=\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta|^{2}}{A}d\alpha+i\int\theta\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}d\alpha

We then obtain the following equality for the
t
derivative of
E_{0}^{\theta}
:

(5.4)

\frac{dE_{0}^{\theta}}{dt}=2Re\left\{\int\frac{D_{t}\bar{\theta}}{A}Gd\alpha\right\}-\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha.

The proof of (
5.4
) is straightforward and can be found in detail in Lemma 4.1 of
[
50
]
.

Next, we consider the equation for
\sigma=D_{t}\theta
. We have

(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})D_{t}\theta=[-iA\partial_{\alpha},D_{t}]\theta+D_{t}G\overset{\text{def}}{=}\tilde{G},

or equivalently

(\partial_{t}^{2}-ia\partial_{\alpha})(\sigma\circ\kappa)=[-ia\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{t}](\theta\circ\kappa)+\partial_{t}(G\circ\kappa)=i\frac{a_{t}}{a}a\partial_{\alpha}\theta\circ\kappa+\partial_{t}(G\circ\kappa).

Hence,

\tilde{G}=i\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}A\theta_{\alpha}+D_{t}G.

Define the energy functional for
\sigma
as:

E_{0}^{\sigma}(t)=\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma|^{2}}{A}d\alpha+i\int\sigma\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\sigma}d\alpha.

Using the basic energy estimate in (
5.4
), we obtain the following expression for
\frac{d}{dt}E_{0}^{\sigma}
:

(5.5)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{0}^{\sigma}(t)

\displaystyle=2Re\left\{\int\frac{D_{t}\bar{\sigma}}{A}D_{t}Gd\alpha\right\}-\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha

\displaystyle+Re\left\{2i\int\frac{1}{A}D_{t}\bar{\sigma}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)A\partial_{\alpha}\theta d\alpha\right\}.

Next, we consider the equation for higher-order derivatives. Recall the corresponding equations:

(5.6)

\displaystyle(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\theta_{k}=G_{k}^{\theta},

\displaystyle(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma_{k}=G_{k}^{\sigma},

where
1\leq k\leq s
, and

(5.7)

\theta_{k}\overset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\theta,\ \sigma_{k}\overset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\sigma.

The corresponding expressions for
G_{k}^{\theta}
and
G_{k}^{\sigma}
are:

(5.8)

G_{k}^{\theta}=\partial_{\alpha}^{k}G+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]\theta,\quad\quad G_{k}^{\sigma}=\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\tilde{G}+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]\sigma.

Define the
k
-th order energies
E_{k}^{\theta}
and
E_{k}^{\sigma}
as:

(5.9)

E_{k}^{\theta}(t)=\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta_{k}|^{2}}{A}d\alpha+i\int\theta_{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{k}d\alpha,

(5.10)

E_{k}^{\sigma}(t)=\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma_{k}|^{2}}{A}d\alpha+i\int\sigma_{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\sigma}_{k}d\alpha.

From the basic energy estimate (
5.4
), we have:

(5.11)

\frac{d}{dt}E_{k}^{\theta}(t)=Re\left\{\int\frac{2D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{k}}{A}G_{k}^{\theta}d\alpha\right\}-\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta_{k}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha,

and similarly for
\sigma_{k}
:

(5.12)

\frac{d}{dt}E_{k}^{\sigma}(t)=Re\left\{\int\frac{2D_{t}\bar{\sigma}_{k}}{A}G_{k}^{\sigma}d\alpha\right\}-\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma_{k}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha.

The total energy
\mathcal{E}_{s}
is defined as:

(5.13)

\mathcal{E}_{s}(t):=\sum_{k=0}^{s}\left(E_{k}^{\theta}(t)+E_{k}^{\sigma}(t)\right).

We aim to prove the following energy estimate:

Theorem 5.1
.

There exists
\delta>0
such that

(5.14)

\frac{d\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)}{dt}\leq C\epsilon^{2}(1+t)^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+C\epsilon^{4}(1+t)^{-1-\delta}.

It is straightforward to verify that (
5.14
) holds for
0\leq t\leq 1
. Therefore, we assume
t\geq 1
in the remainder of this section.

At first, we have the following estimates.

Lemma 5.1
.

There holds

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{s}\left(\left|\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta_{k}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha\right|+\left|\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma_{k}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha\right|\right)\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s}.

Proof.

Using Proposition
4.3
, we first bound each term in the sum:

(1)

For the first term, we have:

\left|\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta_{k}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha\right|\leq C\left\lVert D_{t}\theta_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}E_{k}^{\theta}.

(2)

For the second term, we have:

\left|\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma_{k}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha\right|\leq C\left\lVert D_{t}\sigma_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}E_{k}^{\sigma}.

Combining these, we conclude the desired bound and complete the proof.
∎

To complete the proof of Theorem
5.1
, it remains to establish the inequality

(5.15)

\sum_{k=0}^{s}\left(Re\left|\int\frac{2D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{k}}{A}G_{k}^{\theta}d\alpha\right|+Re\left|\int\frac{2D_{t}\bar{\sigma}_{k}}{A}G_{k}^{\sigma}d\alpha\right|\right)\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s}+C\epsilon^{4}t^{-1-\delta}.

The proof of (
5.15
) is divided into two parts:

(1)

First, we prove the
s
-order energy estimates for
\theta
:

\frac{dE_{s}^{\theta}}{dt}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s}+C\epsilon^{4}t^{-1-\delta},

(2)

Next, we establish the
s
-order energy estimates for
\sigma
:

\frac{dE_{s}^{\sigma}}{dt}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s}+C\epsilon^{4}t^{-1-\delta}.

The primary challenge in both cases lies in addressing the low-frequency components and components with too many derivatives, particularly the insufficient decay of
\zeta-\alpha
and
\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}^{2}\zeta
. The lower order energy estimate is omitted, as all the difficulties that arise in the low-order energy estimates also appear in the
s
-order energy estimate.

5.1.
Some useful lemmas

First, let us briefly highlight the relationship between several norms frequently used throughout the proof and the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{s}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

There holds

\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}}+\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s+1/2}}+\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}}+\left\lVert\Lambda(\zeta-\alpha)\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}\ln t.

Proof.

Recall that

\mathcal{E}_{s}=\sum_{j=0}^{s}(E_{k}^{\theta}+E_{k}^{\sigma})=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\left(\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta_{k}|^{2}}{A}d\alpha+i\int\theta_{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{k}d\alpha+\int\frac{|D_{t}\sigma_{k}|^{2}}{A}d\alpha+i\int\sigma_{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\sigma}_{k}d\alpha\right).

At first, note that (see Lemma
4.9
for an example)

D_{t}\sigma_{k}=2\partial_{\alpha}^{k}D_{t}^{2}\zeta+error

and thus

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Also, since
(\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)=iD_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}-(A-1)\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}
, we have

\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Similarly, using
\theta=2(\zeta-\alpha)+e
we obtain

\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

When it comes to
\left\lVert\Lambda(\zeta-\alpha)\right\rVert_{H^{s}}
and
\left\lVert\Lambda D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}}
, some extra preparation is needed. Define

\eta_{\theta}^{k}=\frac{I-\mathcal{H}}{2}\theta_{k},\ r_{\theta}^{k}=\frac{I+\mathcal{H}}{2}\theta_{k},\ \eta_{\sigma}^{k}=\frac{I-\mathcal{H}}{2}\sigma_{k},\ r_{\sigma}^{k}=\frac{I+\mathcal{H}}{2}\sigma_{k},\

and

R_{\theta}^{k}=i\int\eta_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha+i\int r_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\eta}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha+i\int r_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha,

R_{\sigma}^{k}=i\int\eta_{\sigma}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\sigma}^{k}d\alpha+i\int r_{\sigma}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\eta}_{\sigma}^{k}d\alpha+i\int r_{\sigma}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\sigma}^{k}d\alpha.

Then it’s clear that

i\int\theta_{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{k}d\alpha=\int|\Lambda\eta_{\theta}^{k}|^{2}d\alpha+R_{\theta}^{k},

i\int\sigma_{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\sigma}_{k}d\alpha=\int|\Lambda\eta_{\sigma}^{k}|^{2}d\alpha+R_{\sigma}^{k}

and

R_{\theta}^{k}=i\int\eta_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha-i\int\bar{\eta}_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}r_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha+i\int r_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha=2Re\left\{i\int\eta_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha\right\}+i\int r_{\theta}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\theta}^{k}d\alpha,

R_{\sigma}^{k}=2Re\left\{i\int\eta_{\sigma}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\sigma}^{k}d\alpha\right\}+i\int r_{\sigma}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{r}_{\sigma}^{k}d\alpha.

We verify that
|R_{\theta}^{k}|\leq C\epsilon\mathcal{E}_{s}+C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t,\ \forall 0\leq k\leq s
. For
k=0
, there holds
r_{\theta}^{0}=0
; for
k\geq 1
, since
(I+\mathcal{H})\theta=0
, we derive that

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}r_{\theta}^{k}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}(I+\mathcal{H})\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\theta=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}[\partial_{\alpha}^{k},\mathcal{H}]\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}^{k-j+1}[\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}]\partial_{\alpha}^{j-1}\theta,

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{\alpha}^{k-j+1}[\zeta_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{j}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Note that

\left\lVert[\zeta_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{k+1}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{k}\theta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}

and, thanks to Lemma
4.6
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert[\partial_{\alpha}^{k+1}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{k+1}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{k+1}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t.

Hence we may conclude that
\left\lVert r_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t
. Which implies

|R_{\theta}^{k}|\leq C\left\lVert\eta_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}r_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+C\left\lVert r_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t.

For
R_{\sigma}^{k}
, we mainly consider the case
k=s
, since others can be handled by the method above. Note that

|R_{\sigma}^{s}|\leq\left\lVert\Lambda\eta_{\sigma}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\Lambda r_{\sigma}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert\Lambda r_{\sigma}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2},

and

\displaystyle r_{\sigma}^{s}=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H})\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}(I+\mathcal{H})D_{t}\theta-\frac{1}{2}[\partial_{\alpha}^{s},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}]\theta-\frac{1}{2}[\partial_{\alpha}^{s},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}[\partial_{\alpha}^{s},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\theta.

Note that, by repeating the process above, one may derive
\left\lVert\Lambda[\partial_{\alpha}^{s},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\theta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert[\partial_{\alpha}^{s},\mathcal{H}]D_{t}\theta\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1/2}\ln t
. For the first term, we only consider the extreme case, i.e. all derivatives apply to
D_{t}\zeta
or
\partial_{\alpha}\theta
. Let

I_{1}=[\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}},\ I_{2}=[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{s+1}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Note that

I_{1}=\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\mathcal{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

and we have, according to Lemma 2.14 in
[
54
]
and Lemma
C.1
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(\Lambda\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta)\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert\left[\Lambda,\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{H^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t+C\epsilon\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}.

Also,

\left\lVert\mathcal{H}\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\theta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}+C\epsilon\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}.

In addition, observe that

\left\lVert I_{2}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\left\lVert I_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Hence we conclude that

\left\lVert\Lambda r_{\sigma}^{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t,

which leads to

|R_{\sigma}^{s}|\leq C\epsilon\mathcal{E}_{s}+C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t.

Finally, we derive

\left\lVert\Lambda\eta_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\lVert\Lambda\eta_{\sigma}^{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{E}_{s}-R_{\theta}^{k}-R_{\sigma}^{k}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{s}+C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t.

For
\left\lVert\Lambda(\zeta-\alpha)\right\rVert_{H^{s}}
, note that
\forall 0\leq k\leq s
,

\left\lVert\Lambda\theta_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=\left\lVert\Lambda r_{\theta}^{k}+\Lambda\eta_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert\Lambda\eta_{\theta}^{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}\ln t.

This implies

\left\lVert\Lambda(\zeta-\alpha)\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+\left\lVert\Lambda e\right\rVert_{H^{s}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}\ln t

and it’s easy to verify
\left\lVert\Lambda e\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\leq C\epsilon\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}+C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln t
by using the estimate we have shown above. Similar estimate holds for
\left\lVert\Lambda D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Fix small
\mu>0
. Define

C(f,g,h)=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}hd\beta.

then there exists
C>0
such that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\left\lVert C(f,g,h)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1-\mu})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(|\alpha|\lesssim t^{1-\mu})}+\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}})\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\ln t

\displaystyle+Ct^{-1+\mu}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}},

and

(5.17)

\displaystyle\left\lVert C(f,g,h)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|\geq t^{1+\mu})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(|\alpha|\gtrsim t^{1+\mu})}+\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}})\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\ln t

\displaystyle+Ct^{-1-\mu}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

Proof.

We first consider the case
|\alpha|\leq t^{1-\mu}
. Write

\displaystyle C(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}\partial_{\beta}hd\beta+R,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi i}\left(\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\leq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2}}+\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2}}\right)\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}\partial_{\beta}hd\beta+R,

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+R

where
\left\lVert R\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\ln t
, according to Lemma
A.3
;
and it’s clear that

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1-\mu})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(|\alpha|\leq\frac{3}{2}t^{1-\mu})}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\ln t.

For
I_{2}
, according to Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2}}\frac{f(\alpha)-f(\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}g(\beta)\partial_{\beta}hd\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1-\mu})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2}}\frac{g(\beta)\partial_{\beta}h}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}d\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2}}\frac{f(\beta)g(\beta)\partial_{\beta}h}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}d\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\right\rVert_{L^{1}(|\alpha|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2})}\left\lVert g\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\right\rVert_{L^{1}(|\alpha|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2})}\left\lVert fg\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{-1+\mu}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

Also, it’s obvious that

\left\lVert g(\alpha)\int_{|\alpha-\beta|\geq\frac{t^{1-\mu}}{2}}\frac{f(\alpha)-f(\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}\partial_{\beta}hd\beta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1-\mu})}\leq Ct^{-1+\mu}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lVert C(f,g,h)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|\alpha|\leq t^{1-\mu})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(|\alpha|\lesssim t^{1-\mu})}+\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}})\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\ln t

\displaystyle\ \ \ +Ct^{-1+\mu}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

Observe that the same proof works for
|\alpha|\geq t^{1+\mu}
, and this completes the proof.
∎

5.2.
Estimate
E_{s}^{\theta}

Lemma 5.4
.

There holds

(5.18)

\left|\int\frac{D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{k}}{A}[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]\theta\right|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s},\ \forall 1\leq k\leq s.

Proof.

Straight calculation yields

[D_{t}^{2},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]=D_{t}[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]+[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]D_{t}.

Note that

[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]=[b\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]=-\sum_{j=1}^{k}C_{j,k}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\partial_{\alpha}^{k-j+1},\quad C_{j,k}:=\frac{k!}{j!(k-j)!},

hence there holds

\displaystyle\left|\int\frac{D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{k}}{A}[D_{t}^{2},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]\theta\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left(\left\lVert D_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{k}C_{j,k}(\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b)\theta_{k-j+1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{k}C_{j,k}(\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b)\sigma_{k-j+1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\right).

Using Lemma
4.13
, we derive that

\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1+\delta_{0}}\ln t,\ \forall 1\leq j\leq s.

If
k-j+1\leq s-2
, we have

\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\theta_{k-j+1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\theta_{k-j+1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}.

Otherwise we have

\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\theta_{k-j+1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\theta_{k-j+1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{s}}.

Similar estimates hold for the other part.

So far we have

(5.19)

\left|\int\frac{D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{k}}{A}[D_{t}^{2},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]\theta\right|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s};

for the other half, we compute

[A\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]=-\sum_{j=1}^{k}C_{j,k}\partial_{\alpha}^{j}A\partial_{\alpha}^{k-j+1}

and the proof is thus finished by applying Proposition
4.2
.
∎

Proposition 5.1
.

There exists
\delta>0
such that

(5.20)

\left|\frac{dE_{s}^{\theta}}{dt}\right|\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}\mathcal{E}_{s}+O(\epsilon^{4}t^{-1-\delta}).

Proof.

We have

\frac{dE_{s}^{\theta}}{dt}=Re\left\{\int\frac{2D_{t}\bar{\theta}_{s}}{A}G_{s}^{\theta}d\alpha\right\}-\int\frac{|D_{t}\theta_{s}|^{2}}{A}\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)d\alpha

where
G_{s}^{\theta}=\partial_{\alpha}^{s}(G_{1}+G_{2})+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{s}]\theta
. The commutator is handled by Lemma
5.4
, while
\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}
is handled by Lemma
5.1
. It suffices to deal with
\partial_{\alpha}^{s}(G_{1}+G_{2})
.

Decompose
G_{1},G_{2}
as (let
f(\alpha):=\zeta-\alpha
, and
\eta:=Im(\zeta-\alpha)
)

\displaystyle G_{1}=-2\left[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\mathcal{H}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\bar{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi}\int\frac{(\eta(\alpha)-\eta(\beta))(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{|\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)|^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta,

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi}\int\frac{(\eta(\alpha)-\eta(\beta))(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

\displaystyle+C\int\frac{(\eta(\alpha)-\eta(\beta))^{2}(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))|\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)|^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta,

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi i}\int\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\pi i}\int\frac{(\bar{f}(\alpha)-\bar{f}(\beta))(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

\displaystyle+C\int\frac{(\eta(\alpha)-\eta(\beta))^{2}(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))|\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)|^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

G_{2}=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}(\zeta_{\beta}-1)d\beta-\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}\overline{(\zeta_{\beta}-1)}d\beta

and we only consider those terms in
\partial_{\alpha}^{s}G_{1},\ \partial_{\alpha}^{s}G_{2}
, as they are most challenging to handle, and their treatment essentially encompasses all the methods we will use:

G_{11}=\frac{2}{\pi i}\int\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(\partial_{\alpha}^{s}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-\partial_{\beta}^{s}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta)}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta,
