\left\lVert\Lambda L_{0}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}}\leq C\epsilon t^{2\delta_{0}}
instead of embedding. Note that each term here is at least quadratic, so the other factor will provide sufficient decay that recovers the
t^{2\delta_{0}}
increase.
. For the last term, note that

\left\lVert\Lambda L_{0}\theta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{L_{0}}}+C\epsilon.

And we conclude that

\left\lVert\Lambda L_{0}D_{t}Q\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{L_{0}}}+C\epsilon\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{L_{0}}}.

Claim that

(6.15)

\frac{t}{2}\left\lVert\Lambda(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})Q\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}\ln(1+t),

which completes the proof. We have, according to
[
50
]
,

(6.16)

\begin{split}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})Q=&-\left[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\bar{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}\right](\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\zeta)+[D_{t}\zeta,\bar{\mathcal{H}}]\Big(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}\Big)+D_{t}\zeta[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\\
&-2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{D_{t}\zeta\cdot\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}\Big)^{2}D_{t}\zeta\cdot\partial_{\beta}\zeta d\beta\\
:=&N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3}+N_{4}+N_{5}.\end{split}

The symbol ’
\cdot
’ here means the inner product of 2d vector. i.e. for
z,w\in\mathbb{C}
,
z\cdot w=Re\{\bar{z}w\}=Re\{z\bar{w}\}
. Since
\left\lVert\Lambda f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}}
, we only need to control
\left\lVert N_{j}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}
. First, note that

N_{1}=\frac{2}{\pi}\int\frac{(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta))(\eta(\alpha)-\eta(\beta))}{|\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)|^{2}}(\bar{\zeta}_{\beta}D_{t}^{2}\zeta)d\beta

and we apply Lemma
A.3
to derive
\left\lVert N_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1}\ln t
. A similar estimate applies to
N_{2}
and
N_{4}
, except that one should use Lemma
4.12
to estimate
\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
7
7

7

Such lemma faces some difficulties when it comes to high order derivative. Namely, when we consider the
s-1
order and use the embedding, we must deal with
[D_{t}\zeta,\bar{\mathcal{H}}]\partial_{\alpha}^{s}(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta)
. For such case, we integrate by parts to move 1 derivative to the core and divide into several cases:
|\alpha|\leq t
,
|\alpha|>t
with
|\alpha-\beta|\leq t/2
and
|\alpha|>t
with
|\alpha-\beta|>t/2
. Using the techniques in energy estimate and vector field estimate, one can handle each cases.
. For
N_{3}
, note that

N_{3}=D_{t}\zeta(I-\mathcal{H})\left(D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)

which implies

\left\lVert N_{3}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H})\left(D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1}\ln t.

Here we have used Lemma
4.6
8
8

8

It’s obvious that such argument doesn’t work for high order derivative case. In such case, one should keep the commutator expression of
N_{3}
and use the method shown before.
. For
N_{5}
, write

\displaystyle N_{5}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}Re\{\bar{\zeta}_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta\}d\beta,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}Re\{D_{t}\zeta\}d\beta+O(\epsilon^{4}t^{-\frac{5}{4}})_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}D_{t}\zeta d\beta+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}d\beta

\displaystyle\ \ \ +O(\epsilon^{4}t^{-\frac{5}{4}})_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle=N_{51}+N_{52}+O(\epsilon^{4}t^{-\frac{5}{4}})_{L^{2}}.

N_{51}
is actually quartic (refer to our treatment for
G_{21}
, which appeared in Chapter 5) and we get
\left\lVert N_{51}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{4}t^{-\frac{5}{4}}
; for
N_{52}
, write

N_{52}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}\zeta_{\beta}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}d\beta+O(\epsilon^{4}t^{-\frac{5}{4}})_{L^{2}}

and we integrate by parts to derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}\zeta_{\beta}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta))^{2}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}d\frac{1}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\frac{(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta))^{2}}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}d\beta,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha))^{2}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)\mathcal{H}\left(D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{H}\left((D_{t}\zeta)^{2}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)(I-\mathcal{H})\left(D_{t}\zeta\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{Re\{D_{t}\zeta\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2}[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}N_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}N_{4}-\frac{1}{2}N_{3}+\frac{1}{2}[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

Note that the commutator can be handled by the method shown above, so we conclude that
\left\lVert N_{5}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1}\ln t
. This completes the proof.
∎

7.
Decay estimates

Due to Lemma
4.10
, we only need to make the decay estimate for
t^{4/5}\leq|\alpha|\leq t^{6/5}
.

Note that

\displaystyle(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})D_{t}\theta

\displaystyle=[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},D_{t}]\theta+D_{t}G,

\displaystyle=-i\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)A\partial_{\alpha}\theta+D_{t}G

and we have

(D_{t}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha})D_{t}\theta=D_{t}G-i\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)A\partial_{\alpha}\theta+i(A-1)\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta

Define
w=D_{t}^{2}\theta
to get

D_{t}w-i\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta=D_{t}G+R^{\prime}.

Let
\chi(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a real smooth bump function such that
\int\chi(\alpha)d\alpha=1
. Fix
\nu>0
, then for
(\alpha,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty)
, define

(7.1)

\tilde{E}(t)=\int w\bar{u}-D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}

where
u(\alpha,t)=\chi\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}
. Note that

\tilde{E}(t)=2\int w\bar{u}-\frac{d}{dt}\int D_{t}\theta\bar{u}-\int b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\bar{u}.

Remark 7.1
.

Similar construction of
\tilde{E}(t)
was also employed in
[
1
]
.
[
1
]
takes
u(\alpha,t)=\nu^{3/2}\chi(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\nu^{3/2}\sqrt{t}})
. We note that, as we have formulated the water wave equations in a simpler manner, our analysis is considerably more straightforward.

Theorem 7.2
.

For
\nu\in[t^{-1/5},t^{1/5}]
,

\tilde{E}(t)=C\sqrt{t}W(\nu t,t)+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}})

where
W(\alpha,t)=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}w(\alpha,t)
and
C
is independent of
t
.

Proof.

For the third term of
\tilde{E}(t)
, it’s obvious that

\left|\int b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\bar{u}\right|\leq\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

For the second term, define
U=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}D_{t}\theta
. We have

\displaystyle\int\partial_{t}(D_{t}\theta\bar{u})=\int\partial_{t}(\chi U)

\displaystyle=\int U\partial_{t}\chi+\int\chi\partial_{t}U,

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{t}}\int\left(\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t}\right)\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)U+\int\chi\partial_{t}U.

and

\left(\int\chi\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)^{2}d\alpha\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=t^{\frac{1}{4}};

similar calculation works for
\chi^{\prime}
, so we get

\frac{d}{dt}\int(D_{t}\theta\bar{u})=\int\chi\partial_{t}U+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{20}}).

We invoke the frequency decomposition technique in Proposition
4.2
here. Write (we use Proposition
4.1
here, by noticing
D_{t}\theta=2D_{t}\zeta+e=2Q_{\alpha}+2R+e
).

D_{t}\theta=2P_{>t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}+2P_{\leq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}+e+2R

and we get

\left\lVert P_{\leq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq Ct^{-1}\left\lVert P_{\leq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1},\ \left\lVert\partial_{t}P_{\leq t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-1}.

Hence (recall that
\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\left\lVert R\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}
)

\int\chi\partial_{t}U=\int\frac{it}{2\alpha}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\bar{u}Q_{\alpha}+\int e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\bar{u}\partial_{t}Q_{\alpha}=2\int\chi\partial_{t}U_{H}+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-\frac{1}{4}})+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{3}{4}})

where
U_{H}=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}P_{>t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha}
. Now we have

\left\lVert\partial_{t}U_{H}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=\left\lVert\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}(P_{>t^{-2}}Q_{\alpha})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{4}{5}+\delta_{0}}\ln t

which implies

\left|\int\chi\partial_{t}U\right|\leq C\epsilon t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4}{5}+\delta_{0}}\ln t\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{8}}.

Now it remains to consider the first term of
\tilde{E}(t)
. Note that

\displaystyle\int w\bar{u}=\int D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}

\displaystyle=\int i\partial_{\alpha}\theta\bar{u}+\int(i(A-1)\partial_{\alpha}\theta+G)\bar{u},

\displaystyle=\int i\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\theta-\frac{4\alpha}{it^{2}}L_{0}\partial_{\alpha}\theta+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}\theta\right)\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle=\int i\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\theta\bar{u}+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle=\int\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta\chi+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)\int\chi+\int\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\alpha,t)-\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)\right)\chi+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle=\sqrt{t}\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)+\int\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\alpha,t)-\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)\right)\chi+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}})

where
\Theta=ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\theta
. Note that according to Lemma
4.3
,

\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)=ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4(\nu t)}}\partial_{\alpha}\theta(\nu t,t)+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{4}{5}+\delta_{0}})=W+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Therefore, it suffices to notice (use Hardy’s inequality)

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\alpha,t)-\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)\right)\chi\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\alpha,t)-\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)\right)\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\alpha-\nu t}\chi d\alpha\right|,

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int\left|\frac{\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\alpha,t)-\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta(\nu t,t)}{\alpha-\nu t}\right|^{2}d\alpha\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int|(\alpha-\nu t)\chi|^{2}d\alpha\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\Theta\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}t^{\frac{3}{4}},

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\frac{1}{t^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\alpha L_{0}+\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}\right)\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\theta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}t^{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle+C\left\lVert\frac{\alpha}{t^{2}}\Theta\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\lesssim|\alpha|\lesssim t^{6/5})}t^{\frac{3}{4}},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{4}+\mu+\delta_{0}},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}.

Here we have used the fact that

\left\lVert(\alpha-\nu t)\chi\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=t^{\frac{3}{4}}\left\lVert\alpha\chi(\alpha)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=Ct^{\frac{3}{4}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

One may infer from the proof that

\tilde{E}(t)=\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}).

According to Theorem
7.2
, if
\tilde{E}(t)\leq C
for some constant
C
which is independent of
\nu
, then we may take
\nu\in[t^{-1/5},t^{1/5}]
to derive
W(\alpha,t)\leq Ct^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for any
t^{4/5}\leq|\alpha|\leq t^{6/5}
.

We now try to control
\|\tilde{E}\|_{L^{\infty}}
. Straight computation yields

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}(t)

\displaystyle=\int\bar{u}\partial_{t}w+w\partial_{t}\bar{u}-D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{u}-\partial_{t}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u},

\displaystyle=\int\bar{u}(D_{t}w-b\partial_{\alpha}w)+b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}-D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{u},

\displaystyle=\int(i\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta+D_{t}G+R^{\prime})\bar{u}-b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}+b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}-D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{u},

\displaystyle=\int(D_{t}G+R^{\prime})\bar{u}-\int(\partial_{t}^{2}+i\partial_{\alpha})\bar{u}D_{t}\theta+\int b(\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}-\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}),

\displaystyle\overset{\text{def}}{=}I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

And

\displaystyle(-\partial_{t}^{2}+i\partial_{\alpha})u

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}\left(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi-\frac{it}{2\alpha}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\chi\right)+i\left(e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi+\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\chi\right),

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\frac{it}{2\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi-\partial_{t}^{2}\chi+\frac{i}{2\alpha}\chi+\frac{t^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\chi+\frac{it}{2\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi-\frac{t^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\chi\right),

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(-\partial_{t}^{2}\chi+\frac{it}{\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi+\frac{i}{2\alpha}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi\right).

We now analyze
I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}
respectively.

Lemma 7.1
.

We have

(7.2)

I_{2}=\int\overline{(-\partial_{t}^{2}+i\partial_{\alpha})u}D_{t}\theta=O(\epsilon t^{-1-\delta})

for some
\delta>0
.

Proof.

Actually, we have

\partial_{\alpha}\chi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)=-\frac{2\partial_{t}\chi}{\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t}}

and thus

\partial_{t}^{2}\chi=-\partial_{t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)\partial_{\alpha}\chi\right]=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)\partial_{t}\partial_{\alpha}\chi+\frac{\alpha}{2t^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi.

This implies (we still use
U=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}D_{t}\theta
)

(7.3)

\displaystyle\int\overline{\partial_{t}^{2}\chi e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}}D_{t}\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi U+\int\frac{\alpha}{2t^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi U,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{1}{t}\partial_{t}\chi U+\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U-\frac{1}{2t^{2}}\int\chi U-\int\frac{\alpha}{2t^{2}}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U,

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4t}\int\left(\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t}\right)\partial_{\alpha}\chi U+\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U-\frac{1}{2t^{2}}\int\chi U-\int\frac{\alpha}{2t^{2}}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U,

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4t^{2}}\int\chi U+\frac{1}{4t}\int\left(\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t}\right)\chi\partial_{\alpha}U-\frac{1}{4}\int\frac{\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\partial_{\alpha}U

\displaystyle-\int\frac{\alpha}{2t^{2}}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U.

Recall that

\partial_{\alpha}U=\partial_{\alpha}(e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}D_{t}\theta)=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\left(\alpha L_{0}+\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}\right)D_{t}\theta,

and, for
\alpha
in the support of
u(\cdot,t)
, we have

\left|\frac{\alpha}{t}-\nu\right|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}},

and we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{4}\int\frac{\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}+\nu\right)^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\partial_{\alpha}U\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}\int\left(\left|\frac{2\alpha}{t}\right|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\right)^{2}\left|\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\right|\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\left|\alpha L_{0}D_{t}\theta+\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}D_{t}\theta\right|,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{2}\sqrt{t}}\int\left|\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\right|\left|\alpha L_{0}D_{t}\theta+\frac{t}{2}\Omega_{0}D_{t}\theta\right|+O(\epsilon t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}.

(one should use the frequency decomposition method in Theorem
7.2
to replace
D_{t}\theta
by its high frequency part). Other terms are treated similarly. Now, combining with (
7.3
) yields

(7.4)

\left|\int\overline{\partial_{t}^{2}\chi e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}}D_{t}\theta\right|\leq C\epsilon t^{-1-\delta}.

Also, observe that

\frac{it}{\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi=-\frac{it(\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t})}{2\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi=\frac{i(\alpha-\nu t)}{2\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}\chi,

hence

\displaystyle\int\overline{\left(\frac{it}{\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi+\frac{i}{2\alpha}\chi\right)e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}}D_{t}\theta

\displaystyle=-i\int\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}\chi e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}D_{t}\theta-i\int\frac{\chi}{2\alpha}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}D_{t}\theta.

Again we use

D_{t}\theta=\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta-\frac{4\alpha}{it^{2}}L_{0}D_{t}\theta+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}D_{t}\theta,

\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta=-\frac{2\alpha}{it}\partial_{t}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta+\frac{2}{it}\Omega_{0}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta

to obtain

\int\overline{\left(\frac{it}{\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi+\frac{i}{2\alpha}\chi\right)e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}}D_{t}\theta=\int\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi U^{*}+\int\frac{\chi}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}U^{*}+O(\epsilon t^{-1-\delta})

where
U^{*}=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta
.

Further computation yields

\displaystyle\int\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi U^{*}+\int\frac{\chi}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}U^{*}

\displaystyle=-\int\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}\right)\chi U^{*}-\int\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U^{*}+\int\frac{\chi}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}U^{*},

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha^{2}}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}\chi U^{*}-\int\frac{\alpha-\nu t}{2\alpha}\frac{8\alpha^{3}}{it^{3}}\chi\partial_{\alpha}U^{*}+O(\epsilon t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}})

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\int\overline{\left(\frac{it}{\alpha}\partial_{t}\chi+i\partial_{\alpha}\chi+\frac{i}{2\alpha}\chi\right)e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}}D_{t}\theta\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}}+Ct^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}U^{*}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(t^{4/5}\leq|\alpha|\leq t^{6/5})},

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

(7.5)

I_{3}=\int b(\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}-\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u})=\frac{i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)|^{2}}{\nu}\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

Proof.

We have

\partial_{t}\bar{u}=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi+\frac{it}{2\alpha}\bar{u}

which implies

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta+\frac{it}{2\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\bar{u},

\displaystyle=-\frac{\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t}}{2\sqrt{t}}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\chi^{\prime}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta-\partial_{t}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\bar{u}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}(\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta)\bar{u}.

Note that

\partial_{t}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta=\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta-\partial_{\alpha}(b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta)

and we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\partial_{t}\bar{u}-\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}

\displaystyle=-2\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}+\partial_{\alpha}(b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta)\bar{u}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\Omega_{0}(\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta)\bar{u}-e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle=-2\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}-\frac{it}{2\alpha}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}\theta+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta})_{L^{2}}

which implies

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=-2\int b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}-\int b\frac{it}{2\alpha}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}\theta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-2\int b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}+i\int|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{t}\chi\partial_{\alpha}\theta+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-2\int b\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

We have used Corollary
4.3
and

\left\lVert\partial_{t}\chi\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\nu+\frac{\alpha}{t}\right)\partial_{\alpha}\chi\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq Ct^{-\frac{1}{20}}

here. Integration by parts and observing that

\partial_{\alpha}\bar{u}=\partial_{\alpha}(e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\chi)=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}\bar{u}

yields

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=2\int b_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\theta\bar{u}+2\int bD_{t}^{2}\theta\partial_{\alpha}\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-2\int\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}bw\bar{u}+2\int bD_{t}^{2}\theta e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}\chi+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-2\int\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}bw\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

since
\left\lVert b_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}
. Apply Corollary
4.3
and define
F=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\zeta
to get

\displaystyle\int\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}}bw\bar{u}

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{it}{2\alpha}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-i\int e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}Fw\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-i\int\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}F(\alpha)-\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}F(\nu t)\right)e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}w\bar{u}

\displaystyle\ \ \ -i\frac{4(\nu t)^{2}}{it^{2}}F(\nu t,t)\int e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=I_{31}-ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4(\nu t)}}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\int e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}w\bar{u}.

It’s clear that

|I_{31}|\leq C\left\lVert\sqrt{t}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{it^{2}}F\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\bar{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4}{5}-1+\frac{1}{4}+\delta_{0}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}.

In addition, note that

\displaystyle ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4(\nu t)}}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\int e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}w\bar{u}

\displaystyle=ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4(\nu t)}}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\int\frac{2\alpha}{it}\bar{F}w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4(\nu t)}}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\frac{2(\nu t)}{it}\bar{F}(\nu t)\int w\bar{u}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +ie^{\frac{it^{2}}{4(\nu t)}}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\int\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}\bar{F}-\frac{2(\nu t)}{it}\bar{F}(\nu t)\right)w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=\frac{i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{2\nu}\int w\bar{u}+I_{32}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

Still, there holds

|I_{32}|\leq C\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\sqrt{t}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{2\alpha}{it}F\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=2iD_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\frac{2(\nu t)}{it}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\nu t,t)\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

\displaystyle=2iD_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\nu t,t)\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

\displaystyle=2iD_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\nu t,t)\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

\displaystyle=2i\frac{|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{\nu}\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

The last part of the proof aims to avoid the appearance of an uncontrollable term. To be specific, if we write

I_{3}=\frac{2i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{\nu}\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

and substitute

\tilde{E}(t)=\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}})

into it, then we must deal with the term
\nu^{-1}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}O(\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}})
, which is hard to analyze since we only know that
\nu^{-1}\leq t^{1/5}
. By absorbing
2\nu t/it
into
\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\nu t,t)
, one can avoid this discussion. Such a trick will be applied in the proof of Lemma
7.3
too, and we will not write it out again. Instead, we will simply use the following argument:

\frac{i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{\nu}\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})=\frac{i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{\nu}\tilde{E(t)}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

Lemma 7.3
.

(7.6)

I_{1}=\int(D_{t}G+R^{\prime})\bar{u}=\frac{2i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)|^{2}}{\nu}\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

for some
\delta>0
.

Proof.

To begin with, we assert that

(7.7)

\left|\int R^{\prime}\bar{u}\right|\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}.

To prove (
7.7
), first recall that

R^{\prime}=-i\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)A\partial_{\alpha}\theta+i(A-1)\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta

and
\left\lVert\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}
, which implies

\left|-i\int\left(\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right)A\partial_{\alpha}\theta\bar{u}\right|\leq C\sqrt{t}\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert A\partial_{\alpha}\theta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}.

Also, we have (use Remark
4.3
)

\left|\int i(A-1)\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta\bar{u}\right|\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{5}{4}-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}\ln t\left\lVert\bar{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\frac{1}{20}+\delta_{0}}\ln t.

Next we consider the term

\int D_{t}G\bar{u}.

We begin with a part of
D_{t}G
, namely,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi i}\int\frac{(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta))(D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}(\zeta-\bar{\zeta})_{\beta}d\beta

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\left(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\right)^{2}(D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{\beta}d\beta.

Recall (
5.28
) and we get

\int I\bar{u}=-\int\frac{t^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}(D_{t}\zeta)|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}).

Repeat the technique that we used in Lemma
7.2
(namely, write
\frac{t^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}=4|D_{t}^{3}\zeta|^{2}+error=4|\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta|^{2}+error
, then multiply
\frac{2\alpha}{it}
to each of them) to obtain

\displaystyle\int I\bar{u}

\displaystyle=-\frac{|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{i\nu}\int w\bar{u}+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=\frac{i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta|^{2}}{\nu}\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{2}t^{-1-\delta}).

We still need to consider another part of
D_{t}G
, i.e.

(7.8)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi i}D_{t}\int\frac{(f(\alpha)-f(\beta))(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\pi i}D_{t}\int\frac{(\bar{f}(\alpha)-\bar{f}(\beta))(D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

where
f(\alpha)=\zeta-\alpha
.

We only consider

\tilde{G}_{12}^{*}=\frac{1}{\pi i}\int\frac{(\bar{f}(\alpha)-\bar{f}(\beta))(D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{2}}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta d\beta

since others are similar or easier.

Claim that

(7.9)

\left|\int\tilde{G}_{12}^{*}\bar{u}\right|\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}

for some
\delta>0
.

For
\delta>0
, define

f_{L}=P_{<t^{-1/2-\delta}}f,\ f_{H}=f-f_{L}.

According to the relevant conclusions in Chapter 5, we get

\displaystyle\int\tilde{G}_{12}^{*}\bar{u}

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{it^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}(D_{t}\bar{\zeta})_{H}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta}(\zeta_{\alpha}-1)e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\chi+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{t}{\alpha}(D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta})^{2}(\zeta_{\alpha}-1)e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}\chi+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{t}{\alpha}e^{\frac{it^{2}}{2\alpha}}U^{2}V\chi+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=C\int e^{\frac{it^{2}}{2\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\alpha}{t}U^{2}V\chi\right)+O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta}),

\displaystyle=O(\epsilon^{3}t^{-1-\delta})

as desired. Here

U=e^{-\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}D_{t}^{2}\bar{\zeta},\ V=e^{\frac{it^{2}}{4\alpha}}(\zeta_{\alpha}-1).

This completes the proof of (
7.6
).
∎

Combining Lemma
7.1
,
7.2
and
7.3
yields

Theorem 7.5
.

There exists
\delta>0
such that

(7.10)

\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}(t)=\frac{3i|D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)|^{2}}{\nu}\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{3}(1+t)^{-1-\delta}).

Corollary 7.1
.

(7.11)

\left\lVert\tilde{E}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left\lVert\tilde{E}(0)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\frac{C\epsilon^{3}}{\delta}(1-(1+t)^{-\delta}).

Proof.

Set
f(t)=\frac{3|D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\nu t,t)|^{2}}{\nu}\geq 0
and we get (we still replace
t
by
(1+t)
here)

\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}(t)=if(t)\tilde{E}(t)+O(\epsilon^{3}(1+t)^{-1-\delta})

i.e.

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\left(e^{-i\int_{0}^{t}f(s)ds}\tilde{E}(t)\right)\right|\leq C\epsilon^{3}(1+t)^{-1-\delta}

which implies (
7.11
).
∎

Corollary 7.2
.

For
t^{4/5}\leq|\alpha|\leq t^{6/5}
, there holds

(7.12)

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\theta(\alpha,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq 4\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Combining Corollary
7.1
with Theorem
7.2
yields

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\theta(\nu t,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq 4\epsilon t^{-\frac{1}{2}};

note that the right-hand side is independent of
\nu
and we are done.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

For
t^{4/5}\leq|\alpha|\leq t^{6/5}
, there holds

(7.13)

\left\lVert(D_{t}^{2}\theta,\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\theta,\theta_{\alpha})\right\rVert_{W^{s-2,\infty}\times W^{s-3,\infty}\times W^{s-2,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

One simply repeats the process for
D_{t}^{2}\theta
as above (note that we didn’t lose any derivatives while taking
L^{\infty}
norm, so the process closes). One should pay attention that when it comes to higher order derivatives of the equation, there is an extra term

\int[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{k}]\theta\bar{u}\ (1\leq k\leq s-2).

For such a term, we explicitly compute the commutator (refer to Lemma
5.4
) and treat it by the techniques that were shown in the proof of Lemma
7.2
and Lemma
7.3
.
∎

Appendix A
Singular integrals and commutators

Let
m\geq 1
be an integer. Denote

(A.1)

S_{1}(A,f)=p.v.\int\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{A_{j}(\alpha)-A_{j}(\beta)}{\gamma_{j}(\alpha)-\gamma_{j}(\beta)}\frac{f(\beta)}{\gamma_{0}(\alpha)-\gamma_{0}(\beta)}d\beta.

(A.2)

S_{2}(A,f)=\int\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{A_{j}(\alpha)-A_{j}(\beta)}{\gamma_{j}(\alpha)-\gamma_{j}(\beta)}f_{\beta}(\beta)d\beta.

We have the following commutator estimates, which can be found in
[
42
]
,
[
50
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

We have the following results:

(1) Assume each
\gamma_{j}(j=0,...,m)
satisfies

(A.3)

C_{0,j}|\alpha-\beta|\leq|\gamma_{j}(\alpha)-\gamma_{j}(\beta)|\leq C_{1,j}|\alpha-\beta|.

Then both
\|S_{1}(A,f)\|_{L^{2}}
and
\|S_{2}(A,f)\|_{L^{2}}
are bounded by

C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|A_{j}^{\prime}\|_{X_{j}}\|f\|_{X_{0}},

where one of the
X_{0},X_{1},...X_{m}
is equal to
L^{2}
and the rest are
L^{\infty}
. The constant
C
depends on
\|\gamma_{j}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-1},j=0,1,..,m
.

(2) Let
s\geq 3
be given, and assume (
A.3
) for each
\gamma_{j}
, then

\|S_{2}(A,f)\|_{H^{s}}\leq C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|A_{j}^{\prime}\|_{Y_{j}}\|f\|_{Z},

where for all
j=1,...,m
,
Y_{j}=H^{s-1}
or
W^{[s-1]+1,\infty}
and
Z=H^{s}
or
W^{[s]+1,\infty}
. At most one of these
Y_{j},Z
norms is in
H^{k}
(
k=s-1
for
Y_{j}
or
s
for
Z
). The constant
C
depends on
\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\gamma_{j}-1\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}},\ j=1,...,m
.

(3) Assume (
A.3
) for each
\gamma_{j}
, then

\left\lVert S_{1}(A,f)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left\lVert A^{\prime}_{j}\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}+\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left\lVert A_{j}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\ln r+\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left\lVert A_{j}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

for any
r>1
. The constant
C
depends on
\|\gamma_{j}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-1},j=0,1,..,m
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
K
be such that
K
or
(\alpha-\beta)K(\alpha,\beta;t)
is continuous and bounded, and
K
is smooth away from the diagonal
\Delta=\{(\alpha,\beta)|\alpha=\beta\}
. Denote

(A.4)

\bm{K}f(\alpha,t)=p.v.\int K(\alpha,\beta;t)f(\beta,t)d\beta

Then we have

(A.5)

\begin{split}[\partial_{t},\bm{K}]f(\alpha,t)=&\int\partial_{t}K(\alpha,\beta;t)f(\beta,t)d\beta.\\
[\partial_{\alpha},\bm{K}]f(\alpha,t)=&\int(\partial_{\alpha}+\partial_{\beta})K(\alpha,\beta;t)f(\beta,t)d\beta.\\
[L_{0},\bm{K}]f(\alpha,t)=&\int(\alpha\partial_{\alpha}+\beta\partial_{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}t\partial_{t})K(\alpha,\beta;t)f(\beta,t)d\beta+\bm{K}f(\alpha,t)\end{split}

for
f\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{d})
vanisheses as
|\alpha|\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma A.3
.

Suppose that
A_{1}\in L^{2},f\in L^{2}\cap W^{1,\infty},\ \partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\in W^{1,\infty}\ (2\leq i\leq m)
, then for any
t>e
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\int\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{m}(A_{i}(\alpha)-A_{i}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{m}}fd\beta\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert A_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left(\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}+\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\ln t+\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\int\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{m}(A_{i}(\alpha)-A_{i}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{m}}fd\beta\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\int\frac{\prod_{i\geq 2}(A_{i}(\alpha)-A_{i}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{m}}fd\beta\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\int\frac{\prod_{i\geq 2}(A_{i}(\alpha)-A_{i}(\beta))}{(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta))^{m}}A_{1}fd\beta\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert A_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left(\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}+\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\ln t+\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\prod_{i\geq 2}\|\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|A_{1}f\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert A_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left(\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}}+\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\ln t+\prod_{i\geq 2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}A_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Note that we have invoked Lemma
A.1
.
∎

Appendix B
Frequency localization and Bernstein inequality

Let
d
be a positive integer. Fix a real-valued radially symmetric bump function
\phi
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
adapted to the interval
\{\xi:|\xi|\leq 2\}
such that
\phi(\xi)\equiv 1
for
|\xi|\leq 1
and equals
0
for
|\xi|\geq 2
. Let
N
be a dyadic number. Define the Fourier multipliers

(B.1)

\begin{split}&\widehat{P_{<N}f}(\xi):=\phi(\frac{\xi}{N})\hat{f}(\xi),\\
&\widehat{P_{>N}f}(\xi):=\Big(1-\phi(\frac{\xi}{N})\Big)\hat{f}(\xi),\\
&\widehat{P_{N}f}(\xi):=\Big(\phi(\frac{\xi}{N})-\phi(\frac{\xi}{2N})\Big)\hat{f}(\xi),\\
\end{split}

We record the Bernstein inequality as follows, which can be found, for example, on page 333 of
[
39
]
.

Lemma B.1

(Bernstein inequality)
.

Let
s\geq 0
and
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. We have

(B.2)

\left\lVert P_{\geq N}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,s,d}N^{-s}\left\lVert|\nabla|^{s}P_{\geq N}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(B.3)

\left\lVert P_{N}|\nabla|^{\pm s}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sim_{p,s,d}N^{\pm s}\left\lVert|\nabla|^{s}P_{N}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(B.4)

\left\lVert P_{\leq N}|\nabla|^{s}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sim_{p,s,d}N^{s}\left\lVert|\nabla|^{s}P_{\leq N}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(B.5)

\left\lVert P_{\leq N}f\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,s,d}N^{\frac{d}{p}-\frac{d}{q}}\left\lVert P_{\geq N}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(B.6)

\left\lVert P_{N}f\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,s,d}N^{\frac{d}{p}-\frac{d}{q}}\left\lVert P_{N}f\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

Appendix C
Estimate for half derivative

Lemma C.1
.

We have

(C.1)

\left\lVert\Lambda\mathcal{H}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}}.

Proof.

The proof is fairly standard, but we provide the details here for completeness. Let
s\in[0,1]
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Define a family of operators
T_{s+iy}
as follows:

T_{s+iy}:=|\partial_{\alpha}|^{s+iy}\mathcal{H}(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{s+iy})^{-1}.

Clearly,
T_{s+iy}
is analytic on the strip
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
. We compute the following:

(1)

For any
T_{0+iy}
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert T_{0+iy}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=

\displaystyle\left\lVert|\partial_{\alpha}|^{iy}\mathcal{H}(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{H}(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\left\lVert(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}.

(2)

For any
T_{1+iy}
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert T_{1+iy}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}=

\displaystyle\left\lVert|\partial_{\alpha}|^{1+iy}\mathcal{H}(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{1+iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{H}(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{1+iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\left\lVert(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq C\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}},

By Stein’s analytic interpolation theorem,
T_{s+iy}
is bounded on
L^{2}
, for any
(s,y)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
:

\left\lVert|\partial_{\alpha}|^{s+iy}\mathcal{H}(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{s+iy})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}.

In particular, take
y=0
and
s=1/2
and let
g:=(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{1/2})^{-1}f
or equivalently,
f=(I+|\partial_{\alpha}|^{1/2})g
, we obtain

\left\lVert\Lambda\mathcal{H}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}},

as desired.
∎
